Can derive delta by using linearity: H'_p = H_p + u_p where u_i=-1, u_j=+1 (others 0). Also (1-H'_q)= (1-H_q)-u_q. Product = H_p(1-H_q) + u_p(1-H_q) - H_p u_q - u_p u_q.

Index r = p - q + n-1.
We can build a 2x2 matrix for p in {i,j}, q in {i,j}:
- p=i(q=i): u_i=-1, H_i=1: delta = -1*(1-1) - 1*(-1) - (+1) = 1-1=0? Pair i,i old H_i(1-H_i)=0, new 0, yes.

- p=j,q=i: old 0*(1-1)=0; new 1*(1-0)=1; delta+1. Formula: u_j=1, H_i=1: 1*(0) -0* -1 - (1*-1)=+1.
- p=j,q=j: old0 new 0 delta0.
For p=i,q not i/j: delta u_i*(1-H_q) - 0 -0 = - (1-H_q). Index i - q + n-1.
For p=j,q not: delta + (1-H_q). Index j - q + n-1.
For p not i/j, q=i: delta -H_p*u_i = -H_p*(-1)=H_p. Index p - i + n-1.
For p not, q=j: delta -H_p*1 = -H_p. Index p - j + n-1.
Also pairs p not,q not unchanged.

Gradient wrt h_i:
Row: p=i, q=i-d valid for all d such that q∈[0,n-1], d = i-q, q=0..n-1. Contribution derivative (1-h_i) * 1. Sum over q weights w_{i-q+n-1}.
Column: q=i, p such that p-d = i => d=p-i, p=0..n-1. Derivative -h_i * 1. Sum weights w_{p-i+n-1}.
Thus grad_i = (1-h_i)*R_i - h_i*C_i, where R_i = ∑_{q=0}^{n-1} w_{i - q + n-1}, C_i = ∑_{p=0}^{n-1} w_{p - i + n-1}.
Let weights array w length L. R_i is sum of w over indices i-q+n-1 for q=0..n-1 => indices from i+n-1 down to i. So R_i = sum_{r=i}^{i+n-1} w[r]. C_i = sum over p=0..n-1 w[p-i+n-1] => indices n-1-i to 2n-2-i. So C_i = sum_{r=n-1-i}^{2n-2-i} w[r].
We can compute prefix sums of w length L+1. R_i = pref[i+n] - pref[i]. C_i = pref[2n-1-i] - pref[n-2-i]? Check indices inclusive: start = n-1-i, end = 2n-2-i. pref[end+1]-pref[start] = pref[2n-1-i] - pref[n-1-i]. Since L=2n-1, max end when i=0: 2n-2, pref index 2n-1 OK. Good.

Fast gradient function:
```
def lse_obj_grad(x, beta):
    n=_N; c=our_corr(x); m=np.max(c); shifted=c-m; # could clip to avoid overflow
    cl=np.clip(beta*shifted,-700,700); ex=np.exp(cl); s=ex.sum(); w=ex/s
    if s==0: uniform maybe
    # prefix of w
    pref=np.concatenate(([0],np.cumsum(w)))
    L=2*n-1
    R = pref[n:n+2*n-1?]
```
Need R_i for i=0..n-1: pref[i+n]-pref[i]. `R = pref[n:n+n] - pref[0:n]`? For i=0: pref[n]-pref[0]=sum w[0:n]. i=n-1: pref[2n-1]-pref[n-1]=sum w[n-1:2n-1] length n. So R = pref[n:n+n] - pref[0:n]. Since length L=2n-1, pref length L+1=2n. pref[n:n+n] indices n..2n-1 length n.
C_i: start=n-1-i, end=2n-2-i inclusive, pref[end+1]=pref[2n-1-i], pref[start]=pref[n-1-i]. i=0: pref[2n-1]-pref[n-1]; i=n-1: pref[n]-pref[0]. We can compute `starts = n-1 - np.arange(n)` and `ends = 2*n-1 - np.arange(n)` (pref index after last) so C = pref[ends] - pref[starts]? starts = n-1-i (0..n-1); ends=2n-1-i (n-1..2n-1). `C = pref[ends] - pref[starts]`. For i=0: starts n-1, ends 2n-1 yes. For i=n-1: starts0, ends n. Good.
But note C array computed in i order; for i=0 C[0]=pref[2n-1]-pref[n-1]; C[n-1]=pref[n]-pref[0]. We can just use index arrays.
grad = (1-x)*R - x*C.
value = (m + log(s)/beta) * dx.
```
Need dx=2/n.

```
def eval_swap(c,tmp,row1,row0,hint,n,i,j):
    tmp[:] = c
    tmp[i:i+n] += row1
    tmp[j:j+n] += row0
    tmp[n-1-i:n-1-i+n] += hint
    tmp[n-1-j:n-1-j+n] -= hint
    idx1 = n-1
    tmp[idx1] -= 2
    idx2 = n-1 + j - i
    tmp[idx2] += 1
    idx3 = n-1 + i - j
    tmp[idx3] += 1
    newM = int(tmp.max())
    # for secondary sumsq maybe int64
    return newM, tmp
```

Let's brute derive with all pairs formula for binary:
Initial contribution for pair (p,q) old H_p(1-H_q). New H'_p = if p=i 0 elif p=j 1 else H_p. H'_q similar. Delta = H'_p(1-H'_q)-H_p(1-H_q).
Nonzero categories:
p=i: H'=0: delta = -H_i(1-H_q) = -(1-H_q). (H_i=1). Good row_i.
p=j: H'=1: delta = 1*(1-H'_q) - 0 = 1-H'_q. H'_q = if q=i 0, q=j 1, else H_q. So for q=i:1, q=j:0, else 1-H_q. This matches base 1-H_q + adjustments: q=i base 1-H_i=0+1=1; q=j base 1-H_j=1-1=0; else base. Good row_j.
p other q=i: H_p old 1? p other can be 1 or 0, H_q new 0: delta = H_p*1 - H_p*0 = H_p. Good col_i for p not i,j.
p other q=j: H_q new1: delta = H_p*0 - H_p*(1-H_j=1) = -H_p. Good col_j.
p,j? already rows.
p,i? row.
Our column full adds for all p, then we need remove p=i,j contributions from col_i/j because rows already include them. For col_i full p contribution H_p. Remove p=i H_i=1 at idx n-1. Remove p=j H_j=0 no. For col_j full contribution -H_p. Remove p=i -H_i=-1 at idx n-1+i-j (add +1). Remove p=j -H_j=0. Row_j adjustments included. Thus total exact. Good.

Pseudo:
```
def binary_search_one(h_init,n,rng,max_time,temp0,equalize=True):
    h = make_binary(...)
    c = np.array(our_corr(h), dtype=np.int32)
    M = int(c.max())
    cur_sum_sq = int(np.dot(c,c)) # use int64
    row1 = np.asarray(h[::-1], dtype=np.int32) - 1
    row0 = np.asarrray(1 - h[::-1], dtype=np.int32)
    hint = h.astype(np.int32)
    ones = list(np.where(h==1)[0]); zeros = ...; pos1... 
    best_h = h.copy(); best_M=M
    t0=time.time()
    while time.ok:
       # generate candidate
       mode = rng random
       if mode<0.5: choose max_lag candidate
       else: random
       i = ...; j=...

Implementation of continuous pair line search:
Inputs h float, c float, n, p,q (indices), tmin,tmax.
- coeff = zeros(L) float.
- x=h[p], y=h[q].
- Add row p (b != q): indices b=0..n-1 b!=q. But adding b=q and cross separately? For row p b≠q coeff += (1-h[b]). We can add full row array `rp = 1-h[::-1]?` for row p? For row p (a=p), b index from 0..n-1, idx=p-b+n-1. The sequence over b ascending: idx descending. The r interval [p, p+n-1]. Coeff values for b from p? Let's construct arrays: For row p, positions s=0..n-1 correspond b=n-1-s, idx=p+s. coeff += 1 - h[b] for b≠q; cross handled separately. We can add full `1 - h[::-1]` to coeff[p:p+n], then subtract value at q to exclude from linear, then add cross coeff. Similarly row q subtract full.
- Column p: a=0..n-1, idx=a-p+n-1, interval [n-1-p, n-1-p+n]. coeff += -h[a] for a≠q, add cross separately if a=q? For column p (b=p), a=q cross separate. Add full `-h` to coeff[col start], then subtract at a=q.
- Column q: coeff += h[a] for a≠p, full `h`, subtract at a=p.
- Cross: r1=p-q+n-1: linear coeff += x + 1-y; quad +=1. r2=q-p+n-1: coeff += y + 1-x; quad +=1.
But if r1 or r2 fall within the intervals for p/q, we already added row/column linear at those b/q positions and subtracted cross? Need ensure no double count. Let's define method:
Build full contributions excluding cross using arrays:
For row p: for all b, delta t*(1-H_b). For b=q, linear part from this is t*(1-y). But cross also includes t*x + linear? Actually cross total linear x+1-y = x + (1-y). The (1-y) part is included in row p linear if full; the x part comes from column? Wait cross p,q also affected by column p? a? no column p for b=p only. The cross term old x(1-y); new (x+t)(1-y+t). Variation = x t (negative? derivative wrt H_q? old H_p= x, d(1-H_q)= +t? Since H_q decreases, (1-H_q) increases by t; contribution +x t) + (1-y) t (from H_p increase) + t^2. In our sum sources:
- row p (a=p) b=q: u_p(1-H_q) = t(1-y) from full row.
- column q? b=q, a=p: -H_a u_q = -x*(-t)= x t. This will be in column q full (a=p) if not excluded. We were planning column q for a≠p; but cross includes column p? Actually column q (b=q) a=p gives -H_p * (-t) = x t. Good. So cross linear x+1-y comes from row p (1-y) and column q (x). Quad t^2 from -u_p u_q = -t*(-t)=t^2.
Thus if we add full row p, full column q, full row q, full column p, cross will be included linearly by row p and column q (and row q/col p? For q,p cross: row q gives -t(1-x), column p gives -H_q*(-t)? Let's compute q,p: a=q u=-t, b=p u=t. Terms: u_a(1-H_b) = -t(1-x); -H_a u_b = -y t; -u_a u_b = +t^2. Total linear -(1-x+y)?? Direct variation: y(1-x) -> (y-t)(1-x-t)= y(1-x)+ y t + (1-x)t + t^2? Wait earlier expansion: (y-t)(1-x-t)= y(1-x) + y t? Let's expand: (y-t)(1-x-t)= y(1-x) - y t - x t? No: y*(1-x-t) - t*(1-x-t)= y(1-x) - y t - t(1-x) + t^2 = y(1-x) - t(y+1-x)+t^2. Ah sign negative! Direct: q row value decreases, p column complement decreases? If p increases, 1-H_p decreases, so q row p col contribution decreases additionally. So linear = -(y+1-x). Our formula: -t(1-x) - y t = -t(1-x+y). Good. Sources: row q (a=q,b=p): -t(1-x); column p (b=p,a=q): -H_q*t = -y t. Good. So if we add full rows and columns without exclusions, the linear parts for both cross pairs are included; then we only need add quadratic t^2 at the two cross indices. Is there any other overcount? Let's test: If we sum for all a,b with new H = old + u expression using u, the linear part is exactly Σ_a,b [u_a(1-H_b) - H_a u_b] = Σ_a u_a∑b(1-H_b)+Σ_b -H_b∑a u_a? Wait columns term -H_a u_b summed for fixed b: -u_b Σ_a H_a = -u_b*n/2. Row part: u_a(n/2). Since Σu=0, global linear sum zero. Per r, full rows/cols linear is correct; cross linear automatically included. Quadratic term -u_a u_b per pair: only a=p,b=q gives -t*(-t)=+t^2; a=q,b=p gives -(-t)*t=+t^2; diagonal p,p: -t*t? Wait quadratic for pair (p,p): u_p*u_p with negative sign: -t^2. But product H_p(1-H_p) changes: x(1-x) -> (x+t)(1-x-t)= x(1-x) + t(1-2x) - t^2. Our linear row p b=p: t(1-x); column p a=p: -x t; total t(1-2x); quadratic should -t^2. Did we include quadratic for diagonal? Pair (p,p): u_p=u_p same index; product uses H_p(1-H_p), variation includes -u_p^2. Similarly q,q: +? H_q(1-H_q) -> y(1-y) + (-t)(1-2y) - (-t)^2 = linear -t(1-2y), quad -t^2. Our row/col linear includes row p diag t(1-x), col p -x t; row q -t(1-y), col q +y t. Need add -t^2 at indices for a=p,b=p (lag 0 index n-1) and a=q,b=q (same index n-1). Both diagonals at same lag 0 c0. So quad at n-1 has -2 t^2? Let's check: pair p,p and q,q both diagonal entries same index n-1 (d=0), variation includes -t^2 each, total -2t^2. But c0 = sum h_i(1-h_i). Under pair exchange preserving sum, two diagonal terms: x(1-x)+y(1-y) -> (x+t)(1-x-t)+(y-t)(1-y+t). Variation linear? Sum linear +? quadratic -2t^2. Yes.
Also cross p,q and q,p quad +1 each. Thus quad array has:
- index r1 = n-1 + p - q: +1
- index r2 = n-1 + q - p: +1
- index n-1: -2 (if p != q; if p or q? no)
Important! For binary swaps t=-1 (p 1 ->0, q0->1), t^2=1; quadratics should make delta match binary formula. Let's test c0: linear from row/col cross? For binary x=1,y=0, p/q. Linear at c0: row p diag t(0)=0; col p -x t = -1*t? t=-1 -> +1; row q -t(1-y)= -(-1)*1=+1? Wait t=-1, row q b=q: -t(1-y)= +1; col q +y t=0. total linear +2. Quad -2 =>0. Good. Cross r1: row p b=q linear t(1-y)=-1; col q a=p linear +? +x t = -1? Total linear -2; quad +1 -> -1. Good. So full row/col linear + quadratics exact.
Great! This simplifies: For any pair p,q and t, new c = c + t*L_eff + t^2*quad, where L_eff length L computed by adding:
- row p: + (1-h) reversed placed at p:p+n
- row q: - (1-h) reversed placed at q:q+n
- col p: - h placed at n-1-p : n-1-p+n
- col q: + h placed at n-1-q : n-1-q+n
And quad: +1 at p-q, +1 at q-p, -2 at 0.
Let's verify global sum L_eff =0? Row sums: (n/2) - (n/2) cancel, col sums -n/2 + n/2 cancel. Quad sum 0? +1+1-2=0. Good.

This is nice. For 1D pair optimization, we can build L_eff once per pair O(4n) with zeros array. Then evaluate candidate t values: max(c + t*L + t^2*Q). But computing full vector for many t O(L*grid). We can solve more efficiently because Q sparse (three indices). For t scan, we can compute base = c + t*L. Max over all r. For many t, vectorize t array: (L[:,None] + t[None,:]*L[:,None] + quad[:,None]*t[None,:]^2).max(axis=0). L=2n-1 up to 2000, grid 40 ->80k, fine per pair. Over many pairs maybe okay.
But building L_eff for many candidates O(n) each. Still okay.

Can find optimal t for a pair exactly (or near) by evaluating grid + refine. Since objective convex in t, we can do ternary search on continuous interval, evaluating max O(n) per function (not grid). A few dozen eval per accepted pair, each O(n) -> maybe high. Grid with vectorized max might be faster: 32 evals in one numpy operation O(32L) similar but less Python overhead.
Procedure for pair descent:
- Choose p,q. Determine tmin,tmax. We want move mass from p to q maybe p high q low: p decreases, q increases => t negative if h_p'=x+t. But pair could be reversed. We can just set tmin=-x, tmax=1-y (x+h_t within [0,1], y-t in [0,1]). This allows any direction (t can positive if p low q high). But to be meaningful, choose p high q low, expected negative.
- Build L and Q (Q sparse). Maybe sets q? If p==q skip.
- Define candidate t list within [tmin,tmax]. Since objective max and current t=0, optimal maybe near 0. We can use golden-section? Simpler: evaluate `ts = np.linspace(tmin,tmax, 33)` plus maybe optimum near where top active equal. To avoid large steps that worsen, we can restrict step length to e.g. 0.5 initially, then allow full if improvement. Use `max_step=0.5` then 1.0.
- For each t, compute max. Choose best t (including 0). If best max < current max - 1e-10, accept: h[p]+=t, h[q]-=t, c updated exactly via full formula (c + tL + t^2Q). For speed, after building L we can compute c_new = c + tL + t^2Q; assign.
- If no improvement at current max_step, reduce step or try other pairs.
This continuous coordinate descent may find local optimum quickly. Candidate selection:
- Top lags: for each r top, d=r-(n-1). To reduce it, we want adjust pair (p, q=p-d) with p high (h_p>h_q). For continuous, contribution x(1-y). We can randomly choose p among indices with high h and corresponding q low for that lag, plus random pairs.
- Since h continuous, p may not exactly 1, q not 0. Choose p from top 30% values, q among bottom 30%? We can maintain arrays sorted? Simpler: p = rng.choice(np.where(h > h_quantile_high)[0]) and r top ensures q low. Or p random, q random.
- We can also perform greedy for each top lag: compute values `h[H shift]`? For lag d, candidate p where h_p high and h_{p-d} low. Pick p = argmax h_p - h_{p-d} maybe among valid p. This O(n) per top lag. Good. For deterministic descent:
  * For selected top lag r, d = r-(n-1), valid p range max(0,d) .. n-1+min(0,d). Compute `score = h[p] - h[p-d]` (want high p low q). Choose max or random top. Set q=p-d. This pair directly affects lag r (if swapping high to low) and opposite. Good.
  * Also random pair: p high random, q low random.
This can be run many iterations O(n) per candidate plus L O(n). For n=320, n pairs maybe thousands.
- Step size: If objective improves for a large t, update current c/h. If not, maybe try smaller t. Because max is convex, best t will be somewhere. We can evaluate a grid. To speed, we can first evaluate t at e.g. [-0.5,-0.25,-0.1,-0.05,0.05,0.1,0.25,0.5] relative bounds. Choose best; if improves, update. If best is at boundary, next pair maybe.
- Need handle bounds: x+t in [0,1], y-t in [0,1]. If p high q low and t negative, lower bound -x, upper 1-y. If x~1,y~0, t∈[-1,1]. We'll use full range but grid 33 points.
- After update, h may become slightly outside due float; clip tiny and adjust sum? Since pair exchange preserves sum exactly mathematically, but roundoff. We can renormalize occasionally.
- Objective exact C = max(c)*dx. We can update c exactly with float; max may drift. Recompute c via np.correlate occasionally for validation (e.g., every 100 updates or after phase).

Can we solve pair descent to local optimum? Since pair moves preserve sum and bounds, it's like coordinate descent on max. It should reduce max if possible. For continuous parent, it might find better 0.3808. Let's include.

- 0-10s: load, compute best, SLSQP quick maybe.

- Final: best continuous SLSQP/pair at best n, time remaining 100s.
- Return.

def swap_state(i,j):
   # i in ones, j in zeros
   p1=pos1[i]; last=ones[-1]; ones[p1]=last; pos1[last]=p1; ones.pop(); pos1[i]=-1
   p0=pos0[j]; last0=zeros[-1]; zeros[p0]=last0; pos0[last0]=p0; zeros.pop(); pos0[j]=-1

def gen_candidate(max_idx, ones, zeros, h, n, rng, mode):
   if mode == 0 and len(max_idx)>0:
       r = max_idx[rng.rand_int(len(max_idx))]
       d = r-(n-1)
       # optionally try up to 3 random i from ones; need j=i-d valid zero
       for _ in range(6):
           i = ones[rng.randint(len(ones))]
           j = i - d
           if 0 <= j < n and h[j] == 0:
               return i,j
   # random
   i = ones[rng.randint(len(ones)); j=zeros[rng.randint(len(zeros))]

Continuous pair descent function `continuous_pair_descent(h,n,max_seconds, rng, max_step=1.0, restarts?)`:
- Ensure h float feasible.
- c = our_corr(h). M = c.max(); best_h = h.copy(); best_val = M*dx.
- Precompute L? We'll build per candidate. To speed, we can precompute `h_rev = h[::-1]`, `one_minus_h_rev = 1 - h_rev`, but after each update changes two entries; update rev/h arrays accordingly.
Define arrays: `h` float, `h_rev` view? `h[::-1]` is view; if h changes, view changes. `one_minus_h = 1-h` update maybe. For building L, we can use h directly.
For each candidate (p,q):
```
L = np.zeros(Leng, float)
L[p:p+n] += 1 - h_rev  # row p +
L[q:q+n] -= 1 - h_rev  # row q -
L[n-1-p:n-1-p+n] -= h # col p -
L[n-1-q:n-1-q+n] += h # col q +
```
Wait row p + (1-H_b) for b all. `1 - h_rev` where h_rev[s]=h[n-1-s] for b = n-1-s. For interval p:p+n, adding `1 - h_rev` works (b from n-1 down). Row q subtract `1 - h_rev`.
Column p: a from 0..n-1, interval start n-1-p, values h[a]. Add `-h`. Column q: +h.
Quadratic Q: zeros length L; Q[n-1 + p - q] +=1; Q[n-1 + q - p]+=1; Q[n-1]-=2.
But if p==q skip. If p,q indices maybe causing r1/r2 same? only if p=q, no. If r1/r2 equals n-1? only p=q. Good.
Then choose t. But building L of length 2n for each candidate O(n) memory allocation heavy. We can use preallocated `Larr` and set to zero each candidate: `Larr[:] = 0.0` then slice adds. Q can be handled without full array by adding quadratic to max at three indices when evaluating. For grid t, we can compute base = c + t*L for all t, then adjust the three indices for t^2. To find max over all indices with only three quad adjustments, we can compute max_base for each t, and also candidate values at three indices. Since quad negative at n-1, positive at r1,r2. The max may be at r1/r2 due positive quad; need include them. We can do:
```
# Baseline B = c + t*L for each t.
# For t matrix K x L: base_max = B.max(axis=1)
# For r1: val = c[r1] + t*L[r1] + t^2
# For r2: + t^2
# For r0: -2 t^2
new_max = max(base_max, c[r1]+t*L[r1]+t^2, c[r2]+t*L[r2]+t^2, c[r0]+t*L[r0]-2t^2)
```
If r1/r2/r0 overlap with base? base includes no quadratic; taking max of base for all indices (including r1) without quad and corrected values covers; negative at r0: base may overestimate if r0 was max, corrected lower. But taking max(base_max, corrected positive) would include base[r0] without -2t^2, overestimating for negative quad, potentially accepting a t whose true max lower than estimated? Overestimate could cause rejecting? If we use it to select t, overestimate might choose conservative? It could think t worse than true (if base[r0] high but true lower) and miss improvement, but not falsely accept if we recompute true after selection? We can after choosing best t, recompute exact new c/max with true quad to validate. For selection, overestimate okay if conservative? It may underestimate true max if positive quad? We add corrected positives. For r1/r2, base includes value without +t^2; corrected higher, we include max. Good. For r0 negative, base overestimates; but true lower, so overestimate leads to possibly rejecting an improvement. To be safe, for selection we can compute exact for top few t by full evaluation after candidate. That's fine. Use coarse grid to find promising t, then exact eval for top 3 t.
Exact eval for a given t:
```
newc = c + t*L + t*t*Q? We don't want allocate Q full. We can compute `newc = c.copy(); newc += t*L; newc[r1]+=t^2; newc[r2]+=t^2; newc[r0]-=2*t^2; newM = newc.max()`
```
This is O(n) and alloc/copy. For few t per candidate okay.
Grid selection: We can avoid full matrix by selecting t candidates around current optimum. But function call overhead. Could use vectorized to pick best then exact.

```
def exact_feasible(h,n):
   h=np.clip(h,0,1)
   diff = n/2 - np.sum(h)
   # distribute diff to entries with largest slack
   order = np.argsort(np.minimum(h,1-h))[::-1]
   for idx in order:
      amt = diff
      if diff>0: can_add=min(1-h[idx],diff); h[idx]+=can_add; diff-=can_add
      else: can_add=min(h[idx],-diff); h[idx]-=can_add; diff=-? 
      if abs(diff)<1e-14: break
   # ensure exactly by adjusting one interior
   if abs(diff)>0:
      idx interior = argmax(min(h,1-h)); h[idx]-=diff? h += diff? and clip? 
   return h
```
But if h all 0/1 and diff not 0, impossible; but feasible. Use water fill with 60 iterations and then final `h[np.argmax(h*(1-h)) if exists] += n/2 - np.sum(h)` then clip, recompute. Need exact within 1e-12.

Let's define:
```
def project_box_sum(a,n):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    lo = float(np.min(a)) - 1.0
    hi = float(np.max(a)) + 1.0
    # narrow: we need sum target in [0,n]; choose lo = 0 - max(a)? Actually if a large, lambda negative.
    lo = -float(np.max(a)) - 1e-12
    hi = 1.0 - float(np.min(a)) - 1e-12
    target=n/2.0
    for _ in range(80):
       mid=(lo+hi)/2
       s=np.sum(np.clip(a+mid,0,1))
       if s < target: lo=mid
       else: hi=mid
    h=np.clip(a+hi,0,1)
    diff=target - np.sum(h)
    if abs(diff)>1e-12:
       # distribute small to interior
       slack=np.minimum(h,1-h)
       order=np.argsort(-slack)
       for idx in order:
          if diff > 0:
             add=min(1-h[idx],diff); h[idx]+=add; diff-=add
          else:
             sub=min(h[idx],-diff); h[idx]-=sub; diff+=sub
          if abs(diff)<1e-14: break
       if abs(diff)>1e-14:
          idx=int(np.argmax(h*(1-h)))
          h[idx]+=diff
          h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    return h
```
At final, sum may be off by 1e-13; okay.

For binary, c integer; use `np.correlate(h.astype(float), 1.0-h.astype(float), 'full').astype(np.int32)`. The cost okay. Or compute via int8? `np.correlate` doesn't support int8 maybe promotes. Let's implement SLSQP run with beta and maxiter. It uses global `_SLQS_N`, etc.

After SLSQP, project and compute exact c5; if obj lower? Use exact not LSE. Since SLSQP minimizes LSE, exact max could be slightly higher than beta approx? But if lower exact, update. Need also if SLSQP worsens, ignore.
```
def slsqp_smooth(h,n,beta,maxiter=200):
   global _OPT_N,...
   _OPT_N=n; _OPT_DX=2.0/n; _OPT_BETA=beta
   start=project_box_sum(h,n)
   bounds=[(0,1)]*n
   cons=[{'type':'eq','fun':sum_con,'jac':sum_con_jac}]
   try: res=minimize(smooth_obj,start,jac=smooth_grad,method='SLSqp',bounds=bounds,constraints=cons,options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-14,'iprint':0})
   except: return start
   return project_box_sum(res.x,n)
```

def binary_local_search(h_start,n,rng,max_seconds):
    h = make_binary_close(h_start,n,rng).astype(np.int8) # int8 values 0/1
    c = np.asarray(np.correlate(h.astype(np.float64), 1.0-h.astype(np.float64),'full'), dtype=np.int32)

    best_M=M; best_h=h.copy()
    row1 = np.asarray(h[::-1], dtype=np.int32) - 1
    row0 = np.asarray(1 - h[::-1], dtype=np.int32)
    hint = h.astype(np.int32)
    ones = list(np.nonzero(h)[0]); zeros = list(np.nonzero(h==0)[0])
    pos1...
    tmp = np.empty_like(c)
    start=time.time(); t0 = time.time(); last_improve=start

    no_update=0
    max_attempts = 200 # per outer? 
    while max(0.0,(time.time()-start)<max_seconds and remaining()>10):
       # candidate loop
       improved=False
       attempts=0
       # To explore, maybe rebuild max_idx if M changed
       max_idx = np.where(c == M)[0]
       while attempts < 80 and not improved and time ok:
          attempts += 1
          # generate
          if len(max_idx)>0 and rng.rand()<0.7:
             r = max_idx[rng.randint(len(max_idx))]
             d = r-(n-1)
             # try random i
             cand=None
             for _ in range(8):
                 i = ones[rng.randint(len(ones))]
                 j = i - d
                 if 0 <= j < n and h[j] == 0:
                    cand=(i,j); break
             if cand is None:
                 i=ones[rng.randint(len(ones)); j=zeros[rng.randint(len(zeros))]
          else:
             i=ones[rng.randint(len(ones)); j=zeros[rng.randint(len(zeros))]
          # if i==j skip? impossible, but if h wrong? skip.
          if i==j: continue
          # evaluate
          tmp[:]=c
          tmp[i:i+n] += row1
          tmp[j:j+n] += row0
          tmp[n-1-i:n-1-i+n] += hint
          tmp[n-1-j:n-1-j+n] -= hint
          tmp[n-1] -= 2
          tmp[n-1 + j - i] += 1
          tmp[n-1 + i - j] += 1
          newM = int(tmp.max())
          if newM < M:
              # accept
              # optionally compute S? not needed
              c[:] = tmp
              h[i]=0; h[j]=1
              # update arrays
              row1 = np.asarray(h[::-1], dtype=np.int32)-1
              row0 = np.asarray(1-h[::-1], dtype=np.int32)
              hint = h.astype(np.int32)
              swap_state(...)
              M=newM
              curS = int(np.dot(c.astype(np.int64),c.astype(np.int64))) # if tie? maybe compute
              improved=True
          elif newM == M and rng.rand()<0.2:
              newS = int(np.dot(tmp.astype(np.int64), tmp.astype(np.int64)))
              if newS < curS:
                 c[:]=tmp; h swap; update; curS=newS; improved=True # but M same
          else:
              # anneal worsen
              if newM > M:
                 diff=newM-M
                 if diff <= 2:
                    p = math.exp(-diff/T)
                    if rng.random() < p:
                       c[:]=tmp; h swap; update; M=newM; curS=int(dot); improved=True
          # end if
       if improved:
          no_update=0
          T=max(0.5, T*0.999?) maybe keep
          if M < best_M:
             best_M=M; best_h=h.copy()
          last_improve=time.time()
       else:
          no_update +=1
          T *= 0.95
          if T < 0.4:
             # restart perturbation: accept any random move, maybe several
             for _ in range(20): random swap even if worse (update)
             T=1.0
             if no_update>50: break # stop
          # if time no improve for long? maybe return best
    return best_h.astype(float), best_M*(2.0/n)

```
def binary_swap(i,j,h,ones,zeros,pos1,pos0):
   h[i]=0; h[j]=1
   # update ones remove i
   p=pos1[i]; last=ones[-1]; ones[p]=last; pos1[last]=p; ones.pop(); pos1[i]=-1
   p=pos0[j]; last=zeros[-1]; zeros[p]=last; pos0[last]=p; zeros.pop(); pos0[j]=-1
   ones.append(j); pos1[j]=len(ones)-1
   zeros.append(i); pos0[i]=len(zeros)-1
```

def continuous_pair_descent(h_in,n,max_seconds,rng,max_step=0.5, top_count=8):
    h = np.array(h_in,float)
    h = project_box_sum(h,n)
    c = np.asarray(np.correlate(h,1-h,'full'),float)
    M = float(c.max()); dx=2/n; best_M=M; best_h=h.copy()
    Llen=2*n-1
    Larr=np.zeros(Llen,float)
    newc=np.empty(Llen,float)
    start=time.time(); allows= max_seconds
    # Precompute top? recompute each iter
    no_improve=0
    while time.time()-start < allows and remaining()>20:
       # recompute top every 50? top_idx = np.argsort(-c)[:top_count]
       # choose candidate
       if rng.rand()<0.7:
           top_idx = np.argpartition(-c, min(top_count,Llen)) # small? Could be O(L). Use argsort for L <= 2000.
           # maybe pick random among top_count
           r = top_idx[rng.randint(top_count if top_count<Llen else Llen)]
           d = r-(n-1)
           if d>=0:
              pr = np.arange(d,n); qr = pr-d
           else:
              pr = np.arange(0,n+d); qr = pr-d
           if len(pr)==0: p=rng... 
           else:
              score = h[pr] - h[qr]
              # choose max or random top 5
              # to avoid always max, take top min(10) random
              k = min(10, len(pr))
              top_pos = np.argpartition(-score, k-1)[:k]
              pos = top_pos[rng.randint(k)]
              p = int(pr[pos]); q=int(qr[pos])
       else:
           p = rng.randint(n); q = rng.randint(n)
           if p==q: continue
           if h[p] < h[q]: p,q=q,p
       if p==q: continue
       x=h[p]; y=h[q]

Interpolation function:
```
def interp_to(src, src_n, dst_n):
    if src_n == dst_n: return src.astype(float)
    xp = (np.arange(src_n)+0.5)*2.0/src_n
    xq = (np.arange(dst_n)+0.5)*2.0/dst_n
    return np.interp(xq, xp, src)
```
If src fractional, okay.

Make binary close:
```
def make_binary_close(h,n,rng):
    if len(h)!=n: h=interp_to(h,len(h),n)
    h=np.asarray(h,float)
    half=n//2
    if half<=0 or half>=n: raise
    idx=np.argsort(h)[::-1]  # top half
    b=np.zeros(n,float); b[idx[:half]]=1.0
    # But if there are ties, maybe rng tie break? argsort stable? not. Could add tiny noise to break? For parent, deterministic okay.
    return b.astype(np.int32)
```

Let's implement fast subgradient step as a helper to polish if pair descent stalls:
```
def gradient_polish(h,n,max_seconds,rng,beta=50, top_count=5):
   h=project_box_sum(h,n)
   c=corr; M=max; best...
   for it:
      # weights top equal or exponential
      top = np.argsort(-c)[:top_count]
      # weights proportional exp((c-topmax)/tau) maybe
      w = np.zeros(L); vals=c[top]; w[top] = np.exp((vals - vals.max())/10)
      w /= w.sum()
      grad = objective_grad_weights(w,h,n) # O(n) using prefix
      # line search step alpha in [0.1,0.05,...]
      for alpha in [0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01,0.005]:
          h_new = project_box_sum(h - alpha*grad, n)
          c_new = corr(h_new); M_new=max
          if M_new < M -1e-10: accept; break
   return best
```
This can reduce max using broad descent. Gradient with weights O(n) but c O(n^2). We already have SLSQP; maybe duplicate. But SLSQP may be slower to call; gradient polish can run many small steps. However code complexity. SLSQP already does similar. We can skip.

Potential issue: We call `evaluate_erdos_solution(best_h)` at end; if it expects exactly n as len and maybe validates with `np.sum(h)*dx ==1` within strict, our project sum may be 1e-12 off. Let's make final sum exactly by adjusting.
```
def final_fix(h,n):
   h = project_box_sum(h,n)
   # if sum off by <1e-10, adjust an interior element by exact diff without leaving bounds
   diff = n/2.0 - float(np.sum(h))
   if abs(diff) > 0:
       # find idx with room
       if diff > 0:
           idx = int(np.argmax(1.0 - h))
           add = min(1.0 - h[idx], diff)
           h[idx] += add; diff -= add
       else:
           idx=int(np.argmax(h)); sub=min(h[idx], -diff); h[idx]-=sub; diff += sub
       if abs(diff)>1e-15:
           # distribute to top slacks in loop
           order = np.argsort(np.minimum(h,1-h))[::-1]
           for idx in order: ...
   # ensure within bounds
   h = np.clip(h,0,1)
   return h
```
After final clip, sum maybe off; project again but then Diff. Use project_box_sum 100 iterations enough. To make exact within floating, we can use `h[idx] = h[idx] + (target - sum(h))` for one idx if within [0,1]. If sum error small, possible. Use that.
```
def exact_sum(h,n):
  h=np.clip(h,0,1)
  for _ in range(10):
    diff=n/2 - np.sum(h)
    if abs(diff) < 1e-13: break
    if diff>0:
       idx=int(np.argmax(1-h)); h[idx]=min(1,h[idx]+diff)
    else:
       idx=int(np.argmax(h)); h[idx]=max(0,h[idx]+diff)
  return h
```
Because if diff small, extreme fits. If not, project first.

Let's test gradient formula with simple n=2, weights w vector for c=[c_-1,c_0,c_1]. For x=[1,0], objective linear? R_i = sum w_i..; C_i. Wait product is h_p * (1-h_q). Derivative wrt h_i if i is column q: -h_p, not -h_i. For row p=i: derivative (1-h_i). For column q=i: derivative -h_p summed over p. So gradient_i = (1-h_i)*R_i - (sum over p with q=i) h_p * weight. The column coefficient is not -h_i times C_i, but weighted sum of row values h_p for pairs where q=i. That's a cross-correlation with h, not simple scalar times h_i. My earlier gradient formula was wrong! Because objective is bilinear, not separate. Need correct gradient.

Important. Let's derive properly.
c_idx = sum_{p,q: idx=p-q+n-1} h_p (1-h_q).
Derivative wrt h_i:
- Row p=i: terms (1-h_i) for each q valid => (1-h_i) * R_i where R_i=sum_{q} w_{i-q+n-1}.
- Column q=i: terms -h_p for each p valid => - C_i^h where C_i^h = sum_{p} h_p w_{p-i+n-1}.
Not -h_i*C_i. So gradient = (1-h)*R - col_corr, where col_corr is convolution of h with weights.
We can compute col_corr O(n): For each i, sum over p=0..n-1 h_{?} with index p-i+n-1. Let idx = p - i + n-1 => p = idx - n +1 + i. This is a convolution/correlation. We can use direct loop? Need O(n). This is similar to correlation of weights and h. We can compute using FFT or O(n^2)? n=640, SLSQP gradient then O(n^2) if direct loop over p for each i. But maybe still okay? Previous smooth_grad looped over k O(n^2), fine for n=320. Our fast O(n) was wrong. We need correct gradient. We can compute col_corr via FFT or using scipy correlate? It's exactly `np.correlate(h, w, 'full')`? Let's see: col_corr_i = sum_p h_p w_{p-i+n-1}. Let d=p-i; sum_p h_p w_{d+n-1}. This is cross-correlation of h and w with lag? Define r_i = ∑_p h_p w_{p-i+n-1}. If we correlate h with w: np.correlate(h,w) idx = p - q + n-1? We need output indexed by i. Let q=i. Sum_p h_p w_{p-i+n-1} = correlate(h,w) at index i? Because correlate(h,w) idx = p - i + n-1 -> sum_p h_p w_{p - i + (len w -1)}; since len w =2n-1, len-1=2n-2? Wait p-i+n-1, not 2n-2. w length is 2n-1, its valid indices 0..2n-2. np.correlate(h,w) output length n + (2n-1)-1 =3n-2. Not simple. But w is weights length L, h length n; col_corr i is sum over p of h_p w_{p-i+n-1}. This is a cross-correlation with lag offset. We can compute by for each p, add h_p*w shifted by i = p - r + n-1, r index. That's O(n^2) if dense. But maybe we can exploit that weights nonzeros only top lags? In SLSQP with LSE beta large, weights sparse (top few); then O(kn). For beta moderate, many weights. We can use FFT for O(n log n). Let's derive FFT.
We want vector C length n: C[i] = ∑_{p=0}^{n-1} h_p W[i-p], where W[d] = w_{d+n-1} for d in [-(n-1), n-1]. This is convolution of h and W truncated to first n? Specifically (h * W)[i] = ∑_{p} h_p W[i-p]. If W[d] defined for d negatives to positives, then C_i = (h * W)[i]. We need W array length L centered at 0: W[d+n-1]=w[d]. Convolution h (length n) with W (length L) full length 3n-2. C_i = conv_full[i]? Since conv index i = ∑_{p} h_p W[i-p]. Yes, for i=0..n-1. So C = linear_conv(h, W)[0:n]. We can compute with FFT O((n+L)log). L=2n-1, N next pow2(3n-2). For n up to 700, FFT ~2048, fast.
Thus fast smooth gradient:
- R_i = sliding sum of w: R = pref[i+n]-pref[i] as before (valid q).
- W = w (length L), but alignment: W[d+n-1]=w[d]. Convolution `conv = np.real(ifft(fft(h,N)*fft(W,N)))[:n]`. Need ensure orientation. Convolution definition: (a*b)[k]=∑_m a[m]b[k-m]. We need i index from 0. If h length n, W length L, b index d+n-1. Then C_i = ∑_p h_p W[i-p] because w index = (p-i)+n-1? Let's check: C_i = ∑_p h_p w_{p-i+n-1}. Want W[j] = w_{j - ?}. Implement `smooth_grad_correct(x)`:
```
n=_OPT_N; beta=_OPT_BETA; c=np.correlate(x,1-x,'full'); m=max; w...
row=np.zeros(n); col=np.zeros(n)
# Use nz = np.where(w > 1e-12)[0]
for r in nz:
   d = r - (n-1)
   wv = w[r]
   if d >=0:
       nd = n - d
       if nd > 0:
          row[d:] += wv * (1.0 - x[:nd])
          col[:nd] += wv * x[d:]
   else:
       ao = -d
       nd = n - ao
       if nd > 0:
          row[:nd] += wv * (1.0 - x[ao:])
          col[ao:] += wv * x[:nd]
grad = row - col
return grad*_OPT_DX
```
Test row/col ranges:
d>=0: p=i, q=i-d: q=0..n-1-d, i=d..n-1. row[d:d+nd] += (1-x[0:nd]). yes.
Column p=i+d: i=0..nd-1, p=d+d? p = i+d, so x[d:d+nd]. col[:nd]+=x[d:nd? d:n-d]. yes.
d<0 (d=-a): row p=i, q=i+a; valid i=0..n-1-a, q=a..n-1 => row[:nd] += 1-x[a:]. col: q=i, p=i-a valid i=a..n-1, p=0..n-1-a => col[a:] += x[:nd]. Good.

In `continuous_pair_descent`, we use `project_box_sum` several times; if n large, project each 50 accepts. Fine.

Let's detail `continuous_pair_descent` code:
```
def continuous_pair_descent(h_in, n, max_seconds, rng):
    h = np.array(h_in, dtype=float).copy()
    h = project_box_sum(h, n)
    c = np.asarray(compute_corr(h), dtype=float)
    M = float(c.max()); dx=2.0/n
    best_M=M; best_h=h.copy(); curS=float(np.dot(c,c))
    Llen=2*n-1
    Larr=np.zeros(Llen,float)
    newc=np.empty(Llen,float)
    start=time.time(); max_iter=int(min(3000, max(200, max_seconds*30)))
    it=0; no_improve=0
    while time.time()-start < max_seconds and remaining()>20 and it<max_iter:
       it +=1
       # choose candidate
       if rng.random() < 0.75:
           # top 8
           k = min(8, Llen)
           # Use argpartition for speed
           top_idx = np.argpartition(-c, k-1)[:k]
           r = int(top_idx[rng.randint(k)])
           d = r - (n-1)
           if d >= 0:
               np_ = n - d
               if np_ <= 0: p=q=0
               else:
                   pr = np.arange(d, n); qr=pr-d
           else:
               ao=-d; np_=n-ao
               if np_ <=0: p=q=0
               else:
                   pr = np.arange(0,n-ao); qr=pr-d
           if np_>0:
               score = h[pr] - h[qr]
               selk = min(8, np_)
               sel = np.argpartition(-score, selk-1)[:selk]
               pos = sel[rng.randint(selk)]
               p=int(pr[pos]); q=int(qr[pos])
           else:
               p=int(rng.randint(n)); q=int(rng.randint(n))
       else:
           p=int(rng.randint(n)); q=int(rng.randint(n))
           if p==q: continue
           if h[p] < h[q]: p,q=q,p
       if p==q: continue
       x=h[p]; y=h[q]
       tmin=-x; tmax=1.0-y
       if tmax - tmin <= 1e-12: continue
       # Step fractions full range maybe but limit to 1.0? Bounds already <=1.
       # Use fractions of full range:
       fr = np.array([-1.0,-0.75,-0.5,-0.35,-0.25,-0.15,-0.08,0.0,0.08,0.15,0.25,0.35,0.5,0.75,1.0])
       ts = fr * (tmax - tmin) + tmin
       ts = np.unique(np.clip(ts, tmin, tmax))
       # Build L
       Larr[:] = 0.0
       one_minus_rev = 1.0 - h[::-1]
       Larr[p:p+n] += one_minus_rev
       Larr[q:q+n] -= one_minus_rev
       Larr[n-1-p:n-1-p+n] -= h
       Larr[n-1-q:n-1-q+n] += h
       r1 = n-1 + p - q
       r2 = n-1 + q - p
       r0 = n-1
       best_t=0.0; bestM=M; bestS=curS
       for tt in ts:
          tt=float(tt)
          if abs(tt) < 1e-12:
             newM=M; newS=curS
          else:
             newc[:] = c
             newc += tt * Larr
             tt2=tt*tt
             newc[r1] += tt2
             newc[r2] += tt2
             newc[r0] -= 2.0*tt2
             newM=float(newc.max())
             if newM <= M + 1e-9:
                newS=float(np.dot(newc, newc))
             else:
                newS=inf
          if newM < bestM - 1e-11 or (abs(newM - bestM) <= 1e-11 and newS < bestS - 1e-9):
             bestM=newM; bestS=newS; best_t=tt
       if best_t != 0.0 and (bestM < M - 1e-11 or (abs(bestM-M)<=1e-11 and bestS < curS - 1e-8)):
          # update c with best_t
          if abs(best_t) <1e-12: ...
          newc[:] = c
          newc += best_t*Larr
          bt2=best_t*best_t
          newc[r1]+=bt2; newc[r2]+=bt2; newc[r0]-=2*bt2
          c[:] = newc
          h[p] += best_t; h[q] -= best_t
          # tiny clip
          if h[p] < 0: h[p]=0.0
          elif h[p] >1: h[p]=1.0
          if h[q] <0: h[q]=0.0
          elif h[q] >1: h[q]=1.0
          M=bestM; curS=bestS
          if M < best_M - 1e-12:
             best_M=M; best_h=h.copy()
          no_improve=0
          if it % 50 ==0:
             h = project_box_sum(h,n)
             c = np.asarray(compute_corr(h),float)
             M=float(c.max()); curS=float(np.dot(c,c))
             if M < best_M: best_M=M; best_h=h.copy()
             # after recompute, M may differ from bestM due numeric; if increased, continue
       else:
          no_improve +=1
          if no_improve > 300:
             break
    h = project_box_sum(h,n)

def binary_local_search(h_start,n,rng,max_seconds):

def slsqp_smooth(h_start,n,beta,maxiter=200):

Let's refine global helper `thue_morse_binary`:
```
def thue_morse_binary(n):
    v = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    c = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.int64)
    maxv = v.max() if n>0 else 0
    while maxv > 0:
        c ^= (v & 1)
        v >>= 1
        maxv = v.max()
    h = (c & 1).astype(float)
    # adjust to half using make_binary_close? But make_binary_close uses rng; no. Use threshold 0.5? h already 0/1, count may not half.
    half = n//2
    cnt = int(h.sum())
    if cnt > half:
       idx = np.where(h==1)[0]
       h[idx[:cnt-half]] = 0.0 # arbitrary
    elif cnt < half:
       idx = np.where(h==0)[0]
       h[idx[:half-cnt]] = 1.0
    return h
```
If no parent, use random instead maybe.

```
def slsqp_grad(x):
    n=_OPT_N; beta=_OPT_BETA; dxv=_OPT_DX
    x=np.asarray(x,float)
    c=np.correlate(x,1.0-x,mode='full')
    m=np.max(c)
    sh=c-m; cl=np.clip(beta*sh,-700,700); ex=np.exp(cl); s=ex.sum(); w=ex/s if s>0 else np.ones(...)
    row=np.zeros(n); col=np.zeros(n)
    nm1=n-1
    nz = np.where(w > 1e-10)[0]
    for r in nz:
       d = int(r) - nm1
       wv = float(w[r])
       if wv == 0: continue
       if d >=0:
          nd = n-d
          if nd >0:
             row[d:d+nd] += wv * (1.0 - x[0:nd])
             col[0:nd] += wv * x[d:d+nd]
       else:
          ao=-d
          nd=n-ao
          if nd>0:
             row[0:nd] += wv * (1.0 - x[ao:ao+nd])
             col[ao:ao+nd] += wv * x[0:nd]
    return (row-col)*dxv
```
For d>=0, row p=i, q=i-d: i=d..n-1, q=0..n-d-1. row[d:d+nd] += 1-x[:nd]. Yes.
Column p=i+d: i=0..n-d-1, p=d..n-1. col[:nd] += x[d:d+nd]. Yes.
For d<0, ao=-d, row i=0..n-ao-1, q=i+ao=ao..n-1. row[:nd]+=1-x[ao:ao+nd]. Column i=ao..n-1, p=i-ao=0..n-ao-1. col[ao:ao+nd] += x[:nd]. Yes.
Good.

`slsqp_obj` same weights but no need row/col. Use `np.log(s)`; if s==0 impossible.

In objective, if x outside maybe c m large; LSE okay.

Now, final exact sum function. Let's implement `fix_feasible(h,n)` using project and final diff:
```
def fix_feasible(h,n):
    h = project_box_sum(h,n)
    for _ in range(10):
       diff = n/2.0 - float(np.sum(h))
       if abs(diff) < 1e-12: break
       if diff > 0:
          idx = int(np.argmax(1.0 - h))
          if 1.0 - h[idx] <= 0: break
          h[idx] = min(1.0, h[idx] + diff)
       else:
          idx = int(np.argmax(h))
          if h[idx] <= 0: break
          h[idx] = max(0.0, h[idx] + diff)
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    # If sum off due clip, project again with fewer iterations
    diff = n/2.0 - float(np.sum(h))
    if abs(diff) > 1e-12:
       # use water fill local ticks
       order = np.argsort(np.minimum(h, 1-h))[::-1]
       for idx in order:
          if diff > 0:
             add = min(1-h[idx], diff)
             if add <=0: continue
             h[idx] += add; diff -= add
             if abs(diff)<1e-14: break
          else:
             sub = min(h[idx], -diff)
             if sub <=0: continue
             h[idx] -= sub; diff += sub
             if abs(diff)<1e-14: break
    return h
```

```python
import numpy as np
import time
import math
from scipy.optimize import minimize

_START = 0.0
_BUDGET = 1300.0

_OPT_N = 0
_OPT_BETA = 1.0
_OPT_DX = 1.0
_CONS_N = 0
_CONS_TARGET = 0.0


def remaining():
    if _BUDGET <= 0.0:
        return 0.0
    return max(0.0, _BUDGET - (time.time() - _START) - 20.0)


def project_box_sum(a, n):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float).copy()
    if a.size != n:
        a = a[:n] if a.size > n else np.pad(a, (0, n - a.size), mode="constant")
    target = 0.5 * float(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return a
    lo = -float(np.max(a)) - 1.0e-12
    hi = 1.0 - float(np.min(a)) - 1.0e-12
    for _ in range(80):
        mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        s = float(np.sum(np.clip(a + mid, 0.0, 1.0)))
        if s < target:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid
    h = np.clip(a + hi, 0.0, 1.0)
    diff = target - float(np.sum(h))
    if abs(diff) > 1.0e-12:
        order = np.argsort(np.minimum(h, 1.0 - h))[::-1]
        for idx in order:
            if diff > 0.0:
                add = min(1.0 - h[idx], diff)
                if add <= 0.0:
                    continue
                h[idx] += add
                diff -= add
            else:
                sub = min(h[idx], -diff)
                if sub <= 0.0:
                    continue
                h[idx] -= sub
                diff += sub
            if abs(diff) < 1.0e-14:
                break
    return h


def fix_feasible(h, n):
    h = project_box_sum(h, n)
    for _ in range(12):
        diff = 0.5 * float(n) - float(np.sum(h))
        if abs(diff) < 1.0e-13:
            break
        if diff > 0.0:
            idx = int(np.argmax(1.0 - h))
            room = 1.0 - h[idx]
            if room <= 0.0:
                break
            h[idx] = min(1.0, h[idx] + diff)
        else:
            idx = int(np.argmax(h))
            if h[idx] <= 0.0:
                break
            h[idx] = max(0.0, h[idx] + diff)
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    diff = 0.5 * float(n) - float(np.sum(h))
    if abs(diff) > 1.0e-13:
        order = np.argsort(np.minimum(h, 1.0 - h))[::-1]
        for idx in order:
            if diff > 0.0:
                add = min(1.0 - h[idx], diff)
                if add <= 0.0:
                    continue
                h[idx] += add
                diff -= add
            else:
                sub = min(h[idx], -diff)
                if sub <= 0.0:
                    continue
                h[idx] -= sub
                diff += sub
            if abs(diff) < 1.0e-14:
                break
    return h


def interp_to(src, src_n, dst_n):
    src = np.asarray(src, dtype=float)
    if src_n == dst_n:
        return src.copy()
    xp = (np.arange(src_n) + 0.5) * (2.0 / float(src_n))
    xq = (np.arange(dst_n) + 0.5) * (2.0 / float(dst_n))
    return np.interp(xq, xp, src)


def compute_corr(h):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    return np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")


def compute_c5(h):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    n = len(h)
    if n <= 0:
        return float("inf")
    return float(np.max(compute_corr(h)) * (2.0 / float(n)))


def random_binary(n, rng):
    b = np.zeros(max(1, int(n)), dtype=float)
    half = int(n) // 2
    if half > 0 and half < len(b):
        b[rng.choice(len(b), half, replace=False)] = 1.0
    return b


def thue_morse_binary(n):
    n = int(n)
    v = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    c = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.int64)
    if n == 0:
        return c.astype(float)
    while int(v.max()) > 0:
        c ^= (v & 1)
        v >>= 1
    h = (c & 1).astype(float)
    half = n // 2
    cnt = int(np.sum(h))
    if cnt > half:
        idx = np.where(h == 1.0)[0]
        h[idx[:cnt - half]] = 0.0
    elif cnt < half:
        idx = np.where(h == 0.0)[0]
        h[idx[:half - cnt]] = 1.0
    return h


def make_binary_close(h, n, rng):
    n = int(n)
    if len(h) != n:
        h = interp_to(h, len(h), n)
    else:
        h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    half = n // 2
    b = (h > 0.5).astype(np.int32)
    cnt = int(np.sum(b))
    if cnt > half:
        ones = np.where(b == 1)[0]
        bad_order = np.argsort(h[ones])[:cnt - half]
        b[ones[bad_order]] = 0
    elif cnt < half:
        zeros = np.where(b == 0)[0]
        good_order = np.argsort(-h[zeros])[:half - cnt]
        b[zeros[good_order]] = 1
    return b


def slsqp_obj(x):
    n = _OPT_N
    beta = _OPT_BETA
    dxv = _OPT_DX
    c = compute_corr(x)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    shifted = c - m
    clipped = np.clip(beta * shifted, -700.0, 700.0)
    ex = np.exp(clipped)
    s = float(np.sum(ex))
    if s <= 0.0:
        val = m
    else:
        val = m + math.log(s) / beta
    return float(val * dxv)


def slsqp_grad(x):
    n = _OPT_N
    beta = _OPT_BETA
    dxv = _OPT_DX
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    c = compute_corr(x)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    shifted = c - m
    clipped = np.clip(beta * shifted, -700.0, 700.0)
    ex = np.exp(clipped)
    s = float(np.sum(ex))
    if s <= 0.0:
        w = np.ones_like(c) / float(len(c))
    else:
        w = ex / s
    row = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    col = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    nm1 = n - 1
    nz = np.where(w > 1.0e-10)[0]
    if len(nz) == 0:
        nz = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=int)
    for r in nz:
        d = int(r) - nm1
        wv = float(w[r])
        if wv == 0.0:
            continue
        if d >= 0:
            nd = n - d
            if nd > 0:
                row[d:d + nd] += wv * (1.0 - x[0:nd])
                col[0:nd] += wv * x[d:d + nd]
        else:
            ao = -d
            nd = n - ao
            if nd > 0:
                row[0:nd] += wv * (1.0 - x[ao:ao + nd])
                col[ao:ao + nd] += wv * x[0:nd]
    return (row - col) * dxv


def sum_con(x):
    return float(np.sum(x)) - _CONS_TARGET


def sum_con_jac(x):
    return np.ones(_CONS_N, dtype=float)


def slsqp_smooth(h_start, n, beta, maxiter=200):
    global _OPT_N, _OPT_BETA, _OPT_DX, _CONS_N, _CONS_TARGET
    n = int(n)
    h = np.asarray(h_start, dtype=float)
    if len(h) != n:
        h = interp_to(h, len(h), n)
    h = project_box_sum(h, n)
    _OPT_N = n
    _OPT_BETA = float(beta)
    _OPT_DX = 2.0 / float(n)
    _CONS_N = n
    _CONS_TARGET = 0.5 * float(n)
    bounds = [(0.0, 1.0)] * n
    cons = [{"type": "eq", "fun": sum_con, "jac": sum_con_jac}]
    try:
        res = minimize(
            slsqp_obj,
            h,
            jac=slsqp_grad,
            method="SLSQP",
            bounds=bounds,
            constraints=cons,
            options={"maxiter": int(maxiter), "ftol": 1.0e-13, "iprint": 0},
        )
    except Exception:
        return h
    return project_box_sum(res.x, n)


def binary_swap_state(i, j, h, ones, zeros, pos1, pos0):
    h[i] = 0
    h[j] = 1

    p = pos1[i]
    last = ones[-1]
    ones[p] = last
    pos1[last] = p
    ones.pop()
    pos1[i] = -1

    p = pos0[j]
    last = zeros[-1]
    zeros[p] = last
    pos0[last] = p
    zeros.pop()
    pos0[j] = -1

    ones.append(j)
    pos1[j] = len(ones) - 1
    zeros.append(i)
    pos0[i] = len(zeros) - 1


def binary_local_search(h_start, n, rng, max_seconds):
    n = int(n)
    if n % 2 != 0 or n < 4:
        return None, float("inf")

    h = make_binary_close(h_start, n, rng).astype(np.int32)
    hf = h.astype(float)
    c = np.asarray(np.correlate(hf, 1.0 - hf, mode="full"), dtype=np.int32)
    M = int(np.max(c))
    best_M = M
    best_h = h.copy()
    curS = int(np.dot(c.astype(np.int64), c.astype(np.int64)))

    row1 = np.array(h[::-1], dtype=np.int32)
    row1 -= 1
    row0 = np.array(1 - h[::-1], dtype=np.int32)

    ones = list(np.where(h == 1)[0])
    zeros = list(np.where(h == 0)[0])
    pos1 = np.full(n, -1, dtype=np.int32)
    pos0 = np.full(n, -1, dtype=np.int32)
    for p, idx in enumerate(ones):
        pos1[idx] = p
    for p, idx in enumerate(zeros):
        pos0[idx] = p

    tmp = np.empty(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.int32)
    start = time.time()
    T = 2.0
    no_update = 0

    while (time.time() - start < max_seconds) and (remaining() > 15.0):
        max_idx = np.where(c == M)[0]
        improved = False
        attempts = 0
        while (attempts < 140 and not improved and
               (time.time() - start < max_seconds) and (remaining() > 15.0)):
            attempts += 1
            cand = None
            if len(max_idx) > 0 and rng.random() < 0.75:
                r = int(max_idx[rng.randint(len(max_idx))])
                d = r - (n - 1)
                if d != 0:
                    for _ in range(10):
                        i = int(ones[rng.randint(len(ones))])
                        j = i - d
                        if 0 <= j < n and h[j] == 0:
                            cand = (i, j)
                            break
            if cand is None:
                i = int(ones[rng.randint(len(ones))])
                j = int(zeros[rng.randint(len(zeros))])
            else:
                i, j = cand
            if i == j:
                continue

            tmp[:] = c
            tmp[i:i + n] += row1
            tmp[j:j + n] += row0
            tmp[n - 1 - i:n - 1 - i + n] += h
            tmp[n - 1 - j:n - 1 - j + n] -= h
            tmp[n - 1] -= 2
            tmp[n - 1 + j - i] += 1
            tmp[n - 1 + i - j] += 1

            newM = int(np.max(tmp))
            if newM < M:
                c[:] = tmp
                binary_swap_state(i, j, h, ones, zeros, pos1, pos0)
                row1 = np.array(h[::-1], dtype=np.int32); row1 -= 1
                row0 = np.array(1 - h[::-1], dtype=np.int32)
                M = newM
                curS = int(np.dot(c.astype(np.int64), c.astype(np.int64)))
                improved = True
            elif newM == M and rng.random() < 0.25:
                newS = int(np.dot(tmp.astype(np.int64), tmp.astype(np.int64)))
                if newS < curS:
                    c[:] = tmp
                    binary_swap_state(i, j, h, ones, zeros, pos1, pos0)
                    row1 = np.array(h[::-1], dtype=np.int32); row1 -= 1
                    row0 = np.array(1 - h[::-1], dtype=np.int32)
                    curS = newS
                    improved = True
            else:
                if newM > M:
                    diff = newM - M
                    if diff <= 2:
                        if rng.random() < math.exp(-float(diff) / max(0.3, T)):
                            c[:] = tmp
                            binary_swap_state(i, j, h, ones, zeros, pos1, pos0)
                            row1 = np.array(h[::-1], dtype=np.int32); row1 -= 1
                            row0 = np.array(1 - h[::-1], dtype=np.int32)
                            M = newM
                            curS = int(np.dot(c.astype(np.int64), c.astype(np.int64)))
                            improved = True

        if improved:
            no_update = 0
            if T > 1.0:
                T *= 0.999
            if M < best_M:
                best_M = M
                best_h = h.copy()
        else:
            no_update += 1
            T *= 0.92
            if T < 0.35:
                for _ in range(6):
                    i = int(ones[rng.randint(len(ones))])
                    j = int(zeros[rng.randint(len(zeros))])
                    tmp[:] = c
                    tmp[i:i + n] += row1
                    tmp[j:j + n] += row0
                    tmp[n - 1 - i:n - 1 - i + n] += h
                    tmp[n - 1 - j:n - 1 - j + n] -= h
                    tmp[n - 1] -= 2
                    tmp[n - 1 + j - i] += 1
                    tmp[n - 1 + i - j] += 1
                    c[:] = tmp
                    binary_swap_state(i, j, h, ones, zeros, pos1, pos0)
                    row1 = np.array(h[::-1], dtype=np.int32); row1 -= 1
                    row0 = np.array(1 - h[::-1], dtype=np.int32)
                M = int(np.max(c))
                curS = int(np.dot(c.astype(np.int64), c.astype(np.int64)))
                T = 1.5
                no_update = 0
                if no_update > 250:
                    break

    return best_h.astype(float), float(best_M) * (2.0 / float(n))


def continuous_pair_descent(h_in, n, max_seconds, rng):
    n = int(n)
    h = project_box_sum(np.asarray(h_in, dtype=float), n)
    c = np.asarray(compute_corr(h), dtype=float)
    M = float(np.max(c))
    best_M = M
    best_h = h.copy()
    curS = float(np.dot(c, c))
    Llen = 2 * n - 1
    Larr = np.zeros(Llen, dtype=float)
    newc = np.empty(Llen, dtype=float)
    start = time.time()
    max_iter = int(min(3000, max(300, max_seconds * 20)))
    it = 0
    no_improve = 0

    while ((time.time() - start) < max_seconds) and (remaining() > 20.0) and (it < max_iter):
        it += 1
        if rng.random() < 0.75:
            k = min(8, Llen)
            top_idx = np.argpartition(-c, k - 1)[:k]
            r = int(top_idx[rng.randint(k)])
            d = r - (n - 1)
            if d >= 0:
                nd = n - d
            else:
                nd = n + d
            if nd <= 0:
                p = int(rng.randint(n))
                q = int(rng.randint(n))
                if p == q:
                    continue
                if h[p] < h[q]:
                    p, q = q, p
            else:
                if d >= 0:
                    pr = np.arange(d, n, dtype=int)
                    qr = pr - d
                else:
                    pr = np.arange(0, n + d, dtype=int)
                    qr = pr - d
                score = h[pr] - h[qr]
                selk = min(8, len(pr))
                sel = np.argpartition(-score, selk - 1)[:selk]
                pos = sel[rng.randint(selk)]
                p = int(pr[pos])
                q = int(qr[pos])
        else:
            p = int(rng.randint(n))
            q = int(rng.randint(n))
            if p == q:
                continue
            if h[p] < h[q]:
                p, q = q, p

        if p == q:
            continue
        x = float(h[p])
        y = float(h[q])
        tmin = -x
        tmax = 1.0 - y
        if tmax - tmin <= 1.0e-12:
            continue

        Larr[:] = 0.0
        one_minus_rev = 1.0 - h[::-1]
        Larr[p:p + n] += one_minus_rev
        Larr[q:q + n] -= one_minus_rev
        Larr[n - 1 - p:n - 1 - p + n] -= h
        Larr[n - 1 - q:n - 1 - q + n] += h

        r1 = n - 1 + p - q
        r2 = n - 1 + q - p
        r0 = n - 1

        fr = np.array([-1.0, -0.75, -0.5, -0.35, -0.25, -0.15, -0.08,
                       0.0, 0.08, 0.15, 0.25, 0.35, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0], dtype=float)
        ts = fr * (tmax - tmin) + tmin
        ts = np.unique(np.clip(ts, tmin, tmax))

        best_t = 0.0
        bestM = M
        bestS = curS
        for tt in ts:
            tt = float(tt)
            if abs(tt) < 1.0e-12:
                newM = M
                newS = curS
            else:
                newc[:] = c
                newc += tt * Larr
                tt2 = tt * tt
                newc[r1] += tt2
                newc[r2] += tt2
                newc[r0] -= 2.0 * tt2
                newM = float(np.max(newc))
                if newM <= M + 1.0e-9:
                    newS = float(np.dot(newc, newc))
                else:
                    newS = float("inf")

            if newM < bestM - 1.0e-11:
                bestM = newM
                bestS = newS
                best_t = tt
            elif abs(newM - bestM) <= 1.0e-11 and newS < bestS - 1.0e-9:
                bestM = newM
                bestS = newS
                best_t = tt

        if best_t != 0.0 and ((bestM < M - 1.0e-11) or
                              (abs(bestM - M) <= 1.0e-11 and bestS < curS - 1.0e-8)):
            newc[:] = c
            newc += best_t * Larr
            bt2 = best_t * best_t
            newc[r1] += bt2
            newc[r2] += bt2
            newc[r0] -= 2.0 * bt2
            c[:] = newc
            h[p] += best_t
            h[q] -= best_t

            if h[p] < 0.0:
                h[p] = 0.0
            elif h[p] > 1.0:
                h[p] = 1.0
            if h[q] < 0.0:
                h[q] = 0.0
            elif h[q] > 1.0:
                h[q] = 1.0

            M = bestM
            curS = bestS
            no_improve = 0
            if M < best_M - 1.0e-12:
                best_M = M
                best_h = h.copy()

            if it % 50 == 0:
                h = project_box_sum(h, n)
                c = np.asarray(compute_corr(h), dtype=float)
                M = float(np.max(c))
                curS = float(np.dot(c, c))
                if M < best_M:
                    best_M = M
                    best_h = h.copy()
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 900:
                break

    h = project_box_sum(h, n)
    c = np.asarray(compute_corr(h), dtype=float)
    return h, float(np.max(c)) * (2.0 / float(n))


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    global _START, _BUDGET
    _START = time.time()
    _BUDGET = float(budget_s)
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)

    best_h = None
    best_c5 = float("inf")
    best_n = 0

    try:
        parent_h = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float)
    except Exception:
        parent_h = None

    if parent_h is None or len(parent_h) == 0:
        parent_n = 320
        parent_h = thue_morse_binary(parent_n)
    else:
        parent_n = int(len(parent_h))
        if parent_n < 4:
            parent_n = 320
            parent_h = thue_morse_binary(parent_n)

    parent_h = project_box_sum(parent_h, parent_n)
    best_n = parent_n
    best_h = parent_h.copy()
    best_c5 = compute_c5(best_h)

    if remaining() > 80.0:
        scan_base_h = parent_h.copy()
        scan_base_n = parent_n
        scan_ns = set()
        lo = max(160, scan_base_n - 20)
        hi = min(720, scan_base_n + 160)
        for nn in range(lo, hi + 1):
            scan_ns.add(int(nn))
        for extra in [scan_base_n - 2, scan_base_n + 1, scan_base_n + 2,
                      scan_base_n + 3, scan_base_n + 5, scan_base_n + 8,
                      scan_base_n + 12, scan_base_n + 20, 384, 448, 512, 640]:
            if 100 <= int(extra) <= 800:
                scan_ns.add(int(extra))

        scan_scores = []
        for nn in sorted(scan_ns):
            if remaining() < 60.0:
                break
            try:
                h_try = interp_to(scan_base_h, scan_base_n, nn)
                h_try = project_box_sum(h_try, nn)
                c5_try = compute_c5(h_try)
            except Exception:
                continue
            scan_scores.append((float(c5_try), int(nn)))
            if c5_try < best_c5:
                best_c5 = float(c5_try)
                best_n = int(nn)
                best_h = h_try.copy()

        scan_scores.sort()
    else:
        scan_scores = []

    refine_ns = []
    seen_ns = set()
    for item in scan_scores:
        c5_v, nn = item
        if nn not in seen_ns and 120 <= nn <= 750:
            refine_ns.append(int(nn))
            seen_ns.add(int(nn))
        if len(refine_ns) >= 8:
            break
    if best_n not in seen_ns:
        refine_ns.append(int(best_n))
    if parent_n not in seen_ns:
        refine_ns.append(int(parent_n))

    if remaining() < 300.0:
        refine_ns = refine_ns[:1]
    elif remaining() < 600.0:
        refine_ns = refine_ns[:3]
    else:
        refine_ns = refine_ns[:5]

    for nn in refine_ns:
        if remaining() < 45.0:
            break
        try:
            if best_n == nn:
                h_start = best_h.copy()
            else:
                h_start = interp_to(best_h, best_n, nn)
            h_start = project_box_sum(h_start, nn)
            cur_c5 = compute_c5(h_start)
        except Exception:
            continue

        if cur_c5 < best_c5:
            best_c5 = cur_c5
            best_n = int(nn)
            best_h = h_start.copy()

        h_cur = h_start.copy()
        cur_c5 = compute_c5(h_cur)
        if cur_c5 < best_c5:
            best_c5 = cur_c5
            best_n = int(nn)
            best_h = h_cur.copy()

        betas = [30, 150, 600, 2000]
        for beta in betas:
            if remaining() < 25.0:
                break
            if nn <= 420:
                miter = 200
            elif nn <= 650:
                miter = 130
            else:
                miter = 80
            try:
                h_new = slsqp_smooth(h_cur, nn, beta, miter)
                c5_new = compute_c5(h_new)
            except Exception:
                h_new = h_cur
                c5_new = cur_c5
            if c5_new < cur_c5 - 1.0e-13:
                cur_c5 = float(c5_new)
                h_cur = h_new
                if cur_c5 < best_c5:
                    best_c5 = cur_c5
                    best_n = int(nn)
                    best_h = h_cur.copy()

        if remaining() > 70.0:
            cd_time = min(90.0, max(25.0, remaining() * 0.28))
            try:
                h_cd, c5_cd = continuous_pair_descent(h_cur, nn, cd_time, rng)
            except Exception:
                h_cd, c5_cd = h_cur, cur_c5
            if c5_cd < cur_c5 - 1.0e-13:
                cur_c5 = float(c5_cd)
                h_cur = h_cd
                if cur_c5 < best_c5:
                    best_c5 = cur_c5
                    best_n = int(nn)
                    best_h = h_cur.copy()

        if nn % 2 == 0 and remaining() > 90.0:
            bs_time = min(220.0, max(35.0, remaining() * 0.30))
            try:
                h_bin = make_binary_close(h_cur, nn, rng).astype(float)
                h_b, c5_b = binary_local_search(h_bin, nn, rng, bs_time)
            except Exception:
                h_b, c5_b = None, float("inf")
            if h_b is not None and c5_b < cur_c5 - 1.0e-13:
                cur_c5 = float(c5_b)
                h_cur = h_b
                if cur_c5 < best_c5:
                    best_c5 = cur_c5
                    best_n = int(nn)
                    best_h = h_cur.copy()

            if remaining() > 35.0:
                h_frac = project_box_sum(h_b, nn)
                for beta in [200, 800]:
                    if remaining() < 18.0:
                        break
                    try:
                        h_new = slsqp_smooth(h_frac, nn, beta, 100)
                        c5_new = compute_c5(h_new)
                    except Exception:
                        h_new, c5_new = h_frac, cur_c5
                    if c5_new < cur_c5 - 1.0e-13:
                        cur_c5 = float(c5_new)
                        h_frac = h_new
                        if cur_c5 < best_c5:
                            best_c5 = cur_c5
                            best_n = int(nn)
                            best_h = h_frac.copy()
                if remaining() > 25.0:
                    try:
                        h_cd, c5_cd = continuous_pair_descent(h_frac, nn, 30.0, rng)
                    except Exception:
                        h_cd, c5_cd = h_frac, cur_c5
                    if c5_cd < cur_c5 - 1.0e-13:
                        cur_c5 = float(c5_cd)
                        h_frac = h_cd
                        if cur_c5 < best_c5:
                            best_c5 = cur_c5
                            best_n = int(nn)
                            best_h = h_frac.copy()

    if remaining() > 25.0:
        try:
            h_pol = project_box_sum(best_h, best_n)
            c5_pol = compute_c5(h_pol)
        except Exception:
            h_pol = best_h.copy()
            c5_pol = best_c5

        for beta in [300, 1200, 4000]:
            if remaining() < 18.0:
                break
            if best_n <= 420:
                miter = 180
            elif best_n <= 650:
                miter = 110
            else:
                miter = 70
            try:
                h_new = slsqp_smooth(h_pol, best_n, beta, miter)
                c5_new = compute_c5(h_new)
            except Exception:
                h_new, c5_new = h_pol, c5_pol
            if c5_new < c5_pol - 1.0e-13:
                c5_pol = float(c5_new)
                h_pol = h_new
                if c5_pol < best_c5:
                    best_c5 = c5_pol
                    best_h = h_pol.copy()

        if remaining() > 45.0:
            try:
                h_cd, c5_cd = continuous_pair_descent(h_pol, best_n, 75.0, rng)
            except Exception:
                h_cd, c5_cd = h_pol, c5_pol
            if c5_cd < c5_pol - 1.0e-13:
                c5_pol = float(c5_cd)
                h_pol = h_cd
                if c5_pol < best_c5:
                    best_c5 = c5_pol
                    best_h = h_pol.copy()

        if best_n % 2 == 0 and remaining() > 80.0:
            try:
                h_bin = make_binary_close(h_pol, best_n, rng).astype(float)
                h_b, c5_b = binary_local_search(h_bin, best_n, rng, 160.0)
            except Exception:
                h_b, c5_b = None, float("inf")
            if h_b is not None and c5_b < c5_pol - 1.0e-13:
                c5_pol = float(c5_b)
                h_pol = h_b
                if c5_pol < best_c5:
                    best_c5 = c5_pol
                    best_h = h_pol.copy()

    best_h = fix_feasible(best_h, best_n)
    final_c5 = compute_c5(best_h)

    try:
        ev = evaluate_erdos_solution(best_h)
        if isinstance(ev, (tuple, list)):
            ev = ev[0]
        eval_c5 = float(ev)
        if eval_c5 < final_c5:
            final_c5 = eval_c5
    except Exception:
        pass

    return best_h, float(final_c5), int(best_n)
```
